- **2026 papers that bear on benchmark choice and on the LoRA question (evidence, not datasets)** - [E] (a) arXiv 2603.13306 (2026-03-03): on UCF-Crime official test (290 videos, video-level binary, frames at 0.5 fps or 32 uniform frames), UNTUNED Qwen2.5-VL-7B with a basic prompt gets acc 0.8990 / F1 0.9010 / recall 0.8667 / ROC-AUC 0.9011, InternVL3-8B 0.8532 acc; LoRA-tuned 2-4B models land at acc 0.84-0.91 (gemma-3-4b 0.90-0.91, InternVL3-2B 0.87-0.91, Qwen2.5-VL-3B 0.84-0.90 except CoT rows) -> binary anomaly detection on UCF-Crime is near-saturated (about 0.90) and LoRA mainly lifts weak/small baselines to that ceiling. (b) arXiv 2608.21244: 'A VLM Answer Is Not an Anomaly Score' -- decoded answers create ties; probability-weighted scoring over answer options beats decoded-answer scoring by 7.66-19.95 points on the primary metrics across image and video AD benchmarks (relevant to risk_score 0-100 calibration). (c) arXiv 2607.20780: current OWVAD protocols let generic anomaly detection pass as definition-following; negatives are 78.6-96.4% plain normal frames. (d) arXiv 2605.02912 (VANGUARD): LoRA-staged training, UCF-Crime 94% ROC-AUC / 84% F1 in-domain, zero-shot transfer to XD-Violence and ShanghaiTech. (license: n/a (papers). | fit: Informs strategy: public benchmarks would not discriminate between VLMs on binary detection (Qwen2.5-VL-7B untuned already ~0.90) and none exercise the band-calibration failure you measured (S3 36% with 96-98% object confirmation).)
  - Evidence: https://arxiv.org/html/2603.13306 (Tables 2-3, protocol); https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?id_list=2608.21244,2607.20780,2605.02912 (abstracts)
- **Synthetic-vs-real gap and what best complements tierb-v0 [C]** - [C] Your corpus: 450 FLUX.2 1920x1080 stills with declared-truth labels. It measures agreement with the generation spec; Qwen and the flagship both confirm declared objects 96-98% of the time while ~63% of Qwen incident scores sit ~53 points under the declared band, so the S3 gap is a SCORING-RUBRIC/CALIBRATION gap, not a perception gap. No public set measures that: all label 'anomaly yes/no' or free text, none carries a per-image severity band. What real footage adds: (1) real benign frames at real compression/low light/IR/fisheye -> a true S2 (synthetic benign may be too clean, or too staged); (2) real partially-visible incidents; (3) a check that the VLM's conservatism is not a FLUX 'staged-look' artifact. Recommended pair: MEVA (CC BY 4.0, 1080p fixed cameras, abundant benign background + steals/abandons events; the only commercially clean option) as the primary real S2 set, and the UCF-Crime test split (150 normal + 140 in-interval) as an INTERNAL eval-only breadth probe with a legal gate. Everything with NC/ND/DUA terms (CUVA, UBnormal, NWPU, MSAD, UCA, HAWK, XD-Violence, SurveillanceVQA, Vad-R1) should be excluded from training and from any shipped artifact. Per-still severity bands must be authored by us (a small class->band map, then hand-verified); the labels are the product moat, and no downloaded dataset supplies them. (license: See per-item entries; mirrors' CC0/MIT/Apache tags are uploader-asserted and should not be relied on. | fit: Verdict summary: use as-is = MME-RealWorld Monitoring (perception regression only); adapt = MEVA, UCF-Crime test (eval-only), HIVAU intervals; reference = CCTV-Anomaly, CUVA severity curve; not useful/not usable = HAWK, XD-Violence, ShanghaiTech, UBnormal, NWPU, MSAD, SurveillanceVQA, Vad-R1, small still sets.)
  - Evidence: Computed from the findings above and the owner-supplied S1-S5 definitions; MEVA 1920x1080/CC-BY-4.0 (arXiv 2012.00914, mevadata.org); UCF-Crime test composition (datasets-server rows)

**Not verified.**

- Official UCF-Crime license/terms: crcv.ucf.edu fails TLS verification in this sandbox (curl and WebFetch both 'unable to verify the first certificate'); I did not bypass verification. Used a Wayback 2024 capture (no license text found) and the unreachable (000) visionlab.uncc.edu and crcv.ucf.edu/data1 download URLs could not be read. 'No stated terms' is therefore absence in one snapshot, not proof.
- Actual UCF-Crime frame resolution/fps (paper says only 'low resolution'; no videos downloaded). The low-res vs 1920x1080 domain shift is an inference [C].
- Whether backseollgi/UCF-Crime_TEST_SET (15.8 GB usedStorage) is exactly the 290 official test videos (README shows only a tar.gz); mteb/ucf-crime API usedStorage (45.7 GB) disagrees with its tree-file sum (103.8 GB), likely dedup.
- MEVA: per-file sizes, how many person_steals_object / person_abandons_package instances exist inside the public 22 annotated hours, whether more than the two starred activities are 'threat-based' (only two starred in extracted text), residential-site fit, MEVID license, and whether the 2019 AWS size figures (328 h / 516 GB) are still current.
- CCTV-Anomaly (xiaozhang79, gated=auto, created 2026-10-01): card unreadable without login; license of the 8,301 newly collected videos and whether the release is complete are unknown; paper says artifacts 'will be available' at github.com/xiaozhang79/CamVLM (not checked).
- VAGU-T (7,567 videos, 21 categories; arXiv 2608.11260), TAU-Bench (1,118 videos; arXiv 2608.05699) and UCVL (1,699 videos / 16,990 QA, cited by SurveillanceVQA): no usable HF or arXiv hit found in my searches; availability/license unverified.
- CUVA: whether any benign videos exist (all sampled rows were anomalous); exact test video count (~200 inferred from 1,200 rows / 6 tasks); the 10-vs-11 category discrepancy inside the paper/HF prompt. ECVA (CUVA successor, github.com/Dulpy/ECVA) not read.
- MME-RealWorld: licenses of the underlying VisDrone, AI Studio crowd set and LLVIP images; only the Monitoring subset was verified as a surveillance still-image slice. Other general VLM suites (Video-MME, MVBench, etc.) were not verified to have surveillance subsets and are not claimed.
- HF mirror license tags (CC0, MIT, Apache-2.0) and Kaggle CC0 claims are uploader-asserted; a WebSearch summary of Kaggle licenses was not opened/verified.
- Possible contamination: UCF-Crime/XD-Violence/CUVA videos are public web footage and may appear in Qwen3-VL pretraining; not measurable here [?].
- Legal conclusions are my reading of quoted terms, not legal advice; commercial-use clearance (especially for UCF-Crime, XD-Violence, UCA-derived data) needs counsel.
- 'Harbor' (the owner's other suggestion) was out of this track and not researched. HF API rate-limited (HTTP 429) once from shared-IP load; a sibling agent overwrote two of my scratch scripts, so the Hawk file tally was re-run in a private directory (results re-verified).

### EVAL HARNESSES INCLUDING 'HARBOR' (fetched/read 2026-10-03). Tags: [E] external fetched, [C] computed/inferred, [R] repo

**Recommendation.** Keep extending synthbench; do not adopt Harbor. (1) [C] Harbor (harbor-framework/harbor, the Terminal-Bench team's framework, the most plausible meaning) evaluates agents acting in containers. Our unit is one stateless, schema-constrained VLM call whose fidelity depends on the shipped VlmClient (prompt, grammar, /props pin, parser). Harbor would mean 450 container trials, a custom agent wrapping VlmClient, a second scorer and a Python-version split, and it ships no VLM tasks, no Wilson cells and telemetry that is on by default. Harbor fits a different benchmark: evaluating the generation agent or coding agents in containers. (2) The simple benchmark the owner asked for already exists and has produced numbers: tierb-v0 replay with Wilson intervals and an owner audit. A new harness cannot move S3 (36.5% vs the 90% bar): both models confirm the declared objects on 96-98% of stills and the misses are low scores, which is calibration. (3) The real blockers for the LoRA / own-model path are not the harness: (a) no train/held-out split in the export, (b) corpus size, since proving S3 >= 90% needs >=227/241 hits and a 50/50 split leaves ~120 incidents needing ~96% observed, (c) the FLUX.2 [dev] non-commercial licence text, unreadable here (gated), plus the MiniMax-H3 no-improve-other-models clause on clips, and (d) tier fit (VRAM) not being measured on the GB300, a gap every harness shares. (4) If an external harness is ever wanted, choose Inspect AI (MIT): it has ci_wilson, grouped metrics, re-scoring of logs, eval-set multi-model scheduling with resume, openai-api/llama.cpp/vLLM providers with response_schema, and vLLM LoRA adapter serving for A/B. Use lmms-eval (MIT + Apache-2.0) or VLMEvalKit only as an optional side-car for public-benchmark sanity checks on candidate or quantised models, and for its paired-test method. Candidate sweeps across the 10/12/16/24/32/48 GB tiers need only one Model() entry per candidate in synthbench/run/models.py plus serving, about 19-52 min per replay on the GB300.

**Smallest next step.** Add an exact two-sided McNemar p-value to synthbench/score/metrics.py::comparison() (about 10 lines, no new dependency), computed from the discordant counts it already returns, with one unit test and one report.md column; the test fixture is P5a's own 72 vs 22 (expected p about 2.3e-7). That gives paired, significance-tested model-vs-model and LoRA-vs-base deltas on the same 450 items, the one capability worth taking from lmms-eval and Inspect. Immediately after, in order: a seeded, cell-stratified train/held-out split option in `synthbench export vss`, then a read of the FLUX.2 [dev] licence output/training clause by someone with HF access, before any LoRA data is cut.

**Findings.**

- **Harbor = harbor-framework/harbor (Terminal-Bench team). Most plausible referent.** - [E] GitHub 'Framework for evaluating and improving agents'; README: 'from the creators of Terminal-Bench for evaluating and optimizing agents and language models'. Uses: evaluate arbitrary agents (Claude Code, OpenHands, Codex CLI...), 'build and share your own benchmarks and environments', thousands of parallel container environments (Daytona, Modal, ...), generate RL rollouts. It is the official harness for Terminal-Bench-2.0 (`harbor run --dataset terminal-bench@2.0 --agent claude-code --model ...`). Apache-2.0, Python; created 2025-08-04, pushed 2026-10-03; 5,804 stars, 1,922 forks, 979 open issues; PyPI harbor 0.23.0 (2026-09-12), requires-python >=3.12. Unit of work = a task directory (instruction.md + task.toml + environment/ Dockerfile or compose + tests/test.sh that writes /logs/verifier/reward.txt|reward.json); a trial = one AGENT attempt inside a sandbox, then the verifier. Default aggregate = mean reward over tasks (missing reward = 0); custom metric.py allowed. Hub leaderboards 'intentionally do not calculate row scores' (you compute and upload). 85 benchmark adapters in adapters/ (swebench, aider_polyglot, gaia, osworld, mmau, refav, mmmlu, ...). [C] Why most plausible: the owner wrote 'create a simple benchmark, possibly with harbor' and the README sells 'build and share your own benchmarks'. Two other projects share the name: goharbor/harbor (container registry, 29,483 stars, Apache-2.0, 'stores, signs, and scans content') and av/harbor (3,238 stars, Apache-2.0: one-command local LLM stack, llama.cpp/vLLM/Open WebUI/ComfyUI, with a built-in 'Harbor Bench' that is LLM-as-judge for chat/instruction tasks and an lm-evaluation-harness satellite; text-only, no image tasks). av/harbor is the only other plausible reading for a local-GPU user; I cannot confirm which one the owner meant. (license: Apache-2.0 | fit: Does not fit. See next finding.)
  - Evidence: https://api.github.com/repos/harbor-framework/harbor ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/harbor-framework/harbor/main/README.md ; https://pypi.org/pypi/harbor/json ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/harbor-framework/harbor/main/docs-mintlify/core-concepts/tasks/overview.mdx, .../tasks/verifier.mdx, .../harbor-hub/leaderboards.mdx ; https://api.github.com/search/repositories?q=harbor+in:name ; https://api.github.com/repos/av/harbor ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/wiki/av/harbor/5.1.-Harbor-Bench.md
- **Harbor vs our benchmark: paradigm mismatch and integration cost** - [E] Harbor's agents (src/harbor/agents/) are installed CLI agents (claude_code, codex, openhands, aider, goose, gemini_cli, qwen_code, ...), terminus_2 (tmux shell agent over LiteLLM) and computer_1 (desktop screenshot agent); `--model` is a LiteLLM string (its Ollama example runs terminus-2 with ollama/qwen2.5-coder:7b). I found no single-call, model-only runner in that listing. Vision is incidental: ATIF trajectories carry image/audio parts (schema ATIF-v1.8) and LLMJudge forwards trajectory images; none of the 85 adapter NAMES is image/VLM classification (read only mmau, refav, osworld READMEs: mmau converts audio to Qwen2-Audio transcripts 'to adapt the multimodal dataset for text-only LLMs'; refav is code-gen over AV logs; osworld is desktop computer-use). Telemetry to PostHog is on by default (opt out HARBOR_TELEMETRY=off). Podman environment exists (src/harbor/environments/podman.py). Training-adjacent hooks (extras tinker/dspy, `harbor sweeps` = repeat trials with hints, SFT-data export from trajectories) target text-agent trajectories, not local VLM LoRA. [R] Our unit is one stateless call: backend/services/vlm_client.py builds the prompt, sends response_format json_schema (grammar), probes /props for the llama.cpp build pin, parses the verdict (1,034 lines); synthbench/run/AGENTS.md: 'The client is the shipped VlmClient; only its settings and transport differ per model'. [C] To run our replay in Harbor: (a) generate 450 task dirs from the export (shared prebuilt docker_image is allowed); (b) write a custom BaseAgent (`-a module:Class`, runs in the Harbor process) that calls VlmClient, which needs `backend` importable (repo requires-python >=3.14 per pyproject.toml, Harbor >=3.12, so a repo install into Harbor's tool env or a subprocess bridge); (c) a tests/test.sh verifier that applies the s_metrics per-row rule and writes reward.json; (d) a custom metric.py, because the default aggregator averages and counts a missing dimension as 0, while S2 (n=209 benign) and S3 (n=241 incidents) have different denominators and need Wilson cells; (e) 450 container starts for what is one HTTP request each; (f) re-bridge the eval store, audit join and report.html gallery. Estimate ~300-600 lines of glue (by analogy: synthbench/run/replay.py is 364 lines, score/ ~850), for zero new capability. [C] Where Harbor WOULD fit: benchmarking the agents (e.g. the generation agent in synthbench's agent-driven design, or a coding agent working on this repo in a container), which is a different benchmark. Harbor Hub (auth + cloud, `harbor auth login`) is a publishing venue; see the licence finding before putting generated stills anywhere shared. (license: Apache-2.0 | fit: Poor: agent-in-container harness vs single-call VLM classification; adds containers, a second scoring path, telemetry and a Python-version split; no VLM tasks to inherit.)

- **Inspect AI (UK AISI): closest architectural match; the only external harness worth keeping in reserve** - [E] UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai, MIT, 2,925 stars, pushed 2026-10-03, PyPI 0.3.276 (2026-10-02), requires-python >=3.10; inspect_evals 0.23.0 (MIT, 687 stars; README claims 200+ pre-built evals). Task = Dataset (CSV/JSONL/HF; Sample input may be a ChatMessage list with ContentImage) + Solver + Scorer. Metrics: accuracy, stderr, bootstrap_stderr, ci(), ci_wilson() (documented as preferred for binary scores), grouped() per metadata key, clustered SE, custom metrics. `inspect score` re-scores an existing log without re-generating; `inspect eval-set --model a,b` (or per-model --model-spec with own base_url/config) schedules multi-model runs with retry/resume and epochs; `inspect view` is a local log viewer (127.0.0.1:7575). Providers: openai-api (any OpenAI-compatible server, i.e. llama-server), llama-cpp-python, ollama, vllm (can start the server; model name syntax base:adapter loads LoRA adapters, src/.../\_vllm_lora.py), sglang, hf; custom ModelAPI via @modelapi. GenerateConfig.response_schema becomes response_format json_schema (src/inspect_ai/model/\_openai.py:621-627), the same wire VlmClient uses. Its multimodal doc's image-support table lists OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, Mistral, Grok, Bedrock, AzureAI, Groq only; images via local OpenAI-compatible servers are not listed [?]. [C] To keep fidelity we would still wrap VlmClient (custom ModelAPI/solver that ignores Sample.input and calls assess with the item's image paths/detections/timestamp) and a scorer that calls s_metrics: ~200-400 lines by analogy. What it adds over synthbench today: eval-set scheduling/resume/epochs (useful for run-to-run noise: our two Qwen replays read 36.1% and 36.5% S3), vLLM-served LoRA A/B, a generic viewer. What it duplicates: Wilson cells (s_metrics.wilson_interval), slices (score/metrics.py), re-scoring, failure gallery, audit page. Python 3.14 compatibility of inspect-ai is unverified [?] (PyPI classifiers empty; repo needs >=3.14). (license: MIT | fit: Medium-low today (duplicates what we built); best candidate if we outgrow synthbench (many candidates x LoRA variants x epochs needing retries). Does not train anything.)

Task = YAML (HF dataset_path or local data_files via dataset_kwargs, doc_to_visual/doc_to_text/doc_to_messages, process_results/metric_list). v0.6 notes: confidence intervals, clustered SE, baseline-anchored paired comparison with p-value, power-analysis tools, async HTTP eval server; v0.7: per-sample token counts and run throughput. No leaderboard of its own. File-tree keyword scan found no home-security/surveillance task (only MMLU security subjects and cvrr_unusual_and_physically_anomalous_activities, video). Grep of chat/openai.py shows payload['response_format']={'type':'text'} and no json_schema match in openai.py, async_openai.py or vllm.py: it does not drive our grammar-constrained JSON contract [C], so a custom model class wrapping VlmClient is required for fidelity. [C] Integration: local JSONL+images, a task YAML, custom model class, process_results that calls s_metrics: ~150-300 lines; heavy `.[all]` dependency tree. Value: (1) paired tests (the one statistic synthbench lacks, see below), (2) generic-benchmark sanity numbers (e.g. MMMU) to confirm a quantised GGUF or new candidate is not broken. Generic scores do not predict S2/S3 on our schema; synthbench measures that directly. (license: MIT (pipeline) + Apache-2.0 (tasks/models), per LICENSE | fit: Low for the core benchmark; optional side-car for generic sanity checks of candidate/quantised models.)

- **Keep extending synthbench: what exists, what is missing** - [R] Flow: `export vss` -> eval store import (single label authority) -> `replay --model` through the shipped VlmClient (grammar via response_format, /props build pin, enforcement probe; per-model conditions recorded in run.json) -> `score` (S2/S3 only from backend/evaluation/s_metrics.py; Wilson cell for every rate, insufficient under n=10; slices by scenario/group/lighting/weather/property/camera/zone/artifacts; pairwise agreement and discordant lists) + owner audit of 60 stills + report.md/report.html gallery. Adding a sweep candidate = one `Model(...)` entry in synthbench/run/models.py (5 today: qwen3-vl-8b, qwen3-vl-4b, nemotron-12b-vl, cosmos-reason2-8b, flagship; fields name/transport 'ai-vlm'|'vllm'/served_id/url env/default url, optional request_extra, system_message, read_timeout, thinking) plus serving it. Measured replay time on the shared GB300: Qwen3-VL-8B 450 items 18m43s, flagship 52m19s (p5a report), so [C] a 15-candidate sweep is roughly 5 h of replay: wall-clock is not the bottleneck. score/AGENTS.md pins that S2/S3 have one definition; an external harness that re-implements them would create a second one. Gaps [R/C]: (1) comparison() (synthbench/score/metrics.py:195-214) returns agreement and the two discordant lists but no significance test; exact McNemar on P5a's discordant counts (flagship right/Qwen wrong 72, reverse 22) gives p = 2.3e-7 [C], so the test is ~10 lines on data already computed; (2) export/vss.py has no train/held-out split (no split/train/holdout token), mandatory before any LoRA; (3) run_replay has no resume (grep found none), minor at 19-52 min per run; (4) tier fit (VRAM, latency) is not measured: design decision A1 says the GB300 measures accuracy only; the 10/12/16/24/32/48 GB fit must come from real blob sizes + KV + buffers, a gap every external harness shares; (5) stills only, live pipeline is P5b. The P5a result also says the S3 gap is calibration, not perception: both models confirm declared objects on 96-98% of stills and 62.7% of Qwen's incident scores fall below the band by 54 points on average, so a harness swap cannot move S3; prompt/rubric calibration or LoRA on risk scoring can. (license: n/a (in-repo) | fit: Strong: already the shipped-client-faithful, Wilson-reporting, audited harness; gaps are small and local.)

- **Benchmark size, not harness choice, is the binding constraint for proving S2/S3 and for LoRA** - [C] Using s_metrics.wilson_interval's formula (my recomputation reproduces the P5a cell 36.5% [30.7-42.8] at k=88, n=241). To PROVE S3 >= 90% (Wilson lower bound >= 0.90) you need >=227/241 hits (94.2% observed) at n=241, >=115/120 (95.8%) at n=120, >=96/100 at n=100. To PROVE S2 <= 5% (upper bound <= 0.05) you need <=4/209 false positives (1.9%) at n=209, 0/100 at n=100, and no outcome is sufficient at n=60 (0/60 upper = 6.0%). At p=0.9 the interval is [85.6, 93.2] at n=241, [83.3, 94.2] at n=120, [79.9, 95.3] at n=60. [R] s_metrics prints the rate against the bar and states 'nothing in here enforces them', so a 90.0% point estimate would print as a pass while its interval straddles the bar. A 50/50 train/held-out split of tierb-v0 leaves about 120 incidents and 104 benign, so certifying a LoRA'd model needs ~96% observed, or a larger corpus (more stills), plus a split fixed before any training. A harness does not change this arithmetic. (license: n/a | fit: Design input for the corpus/LoRA plan.)
  - Evidence: repo: backend/evaluation/s_metrics.py:29-55 and :32-33 (bars), docs/benchmarks/synthbench/p5a-2026-09-30.md (n=241, n=209, 36.5% [30.7-42.8]); computation: python3 Wilson formula, run in this session

[R] The generation spec states the MiniMax-H3 Community License excludes EU/UK/Korea/USA territory and 'outputs may not be used to improve other AI models'; the owner decided with that known, but it means the H3 clips cannot be LoRA data. [C] Before LoRA on tierb stills, or before uploading the export to Harbor Hub, an HF dataset or any shared registry (a feature of Harbor Hub and a norm for lmms-eval/VLMEvalKit tasks), someone must read the FLUX.2 [dev] licence's output/training terms. The export's still.json sidecar already names that licence. (license: FLUX.2 [dev] non-commercial (text unread) | fit: Blocker to resolve before the LoRA path; irrelevant to local replay scoring.)

**Not verified.**

- Which 'Harbor' the owner means: harbor-framework/harbor is the most plausible, av/harbor (local LLM stack with Harbor Bench, an LLM-as-judge text bench) is the only other plausible reading; goharbor/harbor (registry) is unlikely. Not confirmable from the request.
- https://harborframework.com/docs returned HTTP 307 and was not followed; Harbor behaviour was read from the repo's docs-mintlify/ sources, src tree, README, CHANGELOG and PyPI metadata instead.
- Whether Harbor has a single-call (no-agent) runner: I inspected the src/harbor/agents/ file listing and docs, not `harbor run --help`; I found none. 85 adapter names were listed but only mmau, refav and osworld READMEs were read, so 'no image/VLM-classification adapter' rests on names plus those three.
- Harbor's podman environment (src/harbor/environments/podman.py) exists, but whether it works with synthbench's dedicated rootless podman store (SYNTHBENCH_PODMAN_ROOT) was not tested; per-trial container-start overhead was not measured.
- Python 3.14 compatibility of harbor (>=3.12), inspect-ai (>=3.10), lmms-eval (>=3.10): PyPI classifiers were empty and the repo requires >=3.14 (pyproject.toml:5); no install was attempted (read-only, no downloads).
- Whether Inspect can send images to a local OpenAI-compatible server (llama-server): its multimodal docs table lists only hosted providers; needs a one-sample probe.
- lmms-eval and VLMEvalKit structured-output support rests on greps of three and two files respectively (response_format only found as {'type':'text'} in lmms-eval chat/openai.py); not an exhaustive audit.
- VLMEvalKit leaderboard contents and any VLM scores were not fetched (Space API returned 200 only); OpenVLMRecords was last modified 2025-04-08.
- FLUX.2 [dev] licence output/training terms: LICENSE.txt and README return 401 (gated); only the HF API's license_name 'flux-non-commercial-license' was read.
- The 'existing security benchmark' leads (SurveillanceVQA-589K, Simuletic weapon/knife set, ucf_crime, home-security-detection) are names and download counts only; licences, label schemas and still-vs-video format unverified.
- Line-count integration costs (Harbor 300-600, Inspect 200-400, lmms-eval 150-300) are [C] estimates by analogy to synthbench/run/replay.py (364 lines) and synthbench/score/ (~850 lines), not measured.
- Star counts, release dates and file counts are point-in-time values from 2026-10-03 API fetches.

### LoRA/QLoRA fine-tuning vs training our own model (VLM verdict calibration). Fetched 2026-10-03. Markers: [E] external, f

**Recommendation.** Do not train five models and do not start from scratch. Fix the policy first (cheap, tier-independent), fine-tune only if measured gates say so, and treat training-data licensing as an owner decision before any pixels are used.

STAGE 0 (no training, no new data; about 1-2 days). 0.1 On the GB300, from the existing P5a results (/synthbench/runs/scores/20260930T031706Z/results.jsonl, not present in this sandbox) compute per-scenario risk_score quantiles, the AUROC of risk_score for incident vs benign, and the scenario-grouped-CV S3/S2 frontier of the best monotone remap, for both models. 0.2 One served call on the pinned build to log (a) the order the grammar emits keys and (b) whether logprobs are returned. 0.3 Replay the 450 stills (8B: 18m43s; flagship: 52m19s) with four arms: A shipped prompt; B severity ladder in the prompt (observable criteria, no scenario names); C closed criterion list + code aggregator with a repo-resident policy table; D = B + C. Score on out-of-scenario folds and on a locked set of 4-5 whole scenarios.
DECISION D1 (owner, before any arm ships): approve the severity policy itself (is package theft 'high'? child alone at a pool 'critical'? what is the right band for loitering?) and confirm that 'level of the band midpoint' is the intended S3 bar; decide whether policy lives in prompt, in code, or eventually in weights. Also decide whether the S2/S3 bars are point estimates or Wilson-interval bars (certifying S3>=90% needs 227/241; S2<=5% needs <=4 FP of 209).
GATE G1: if some arm reaches S3 Wilson-lower >= 90% and S2 <= 5% out-of-scenario, STOP. The policy layer is then the product fix for every hardware tier and no fine-tune is needed for this problem.

STAGE 1 (GPU-free, only if G1 fails but AUROC is high): add a calibrator (isotonic/logistic over raw score, verdict, criteria pass-count, detector classes) fit by scenario-grouped CV, shipped as config in the analyzer.

STAGE 2 (only if discrimination or small-tier failures survive Stages 0-1) - SFT-LoRA distillation on Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct (Apache-2.0). Preconditions, DECISION D2 (owner/counsel): (i) training-data licensing: FLUX.2 [dev] stills (reading of FLUX NC v2.1 sections 1(c) vs 2(d); option of a BFL commercial license), and MiniMax-H3 clips excluded outright (V.3 'improve any other AI model', V.4 territory); (ii) budget: 1.5-3K more stills is about 3-7 h of GB300 render time and about 3.5 h of flagship teacher labelling per 3K [C]; (iii) a held-out real-frame test set from the owner's cameras. Recipe: ViT and aligner frozen, LoRA r=16-64 on all linear LLM layers, no modules_to_save, assistant-only loss on the verdict JSON serialized in the grammar's emitted key order, identical chat template and image-then-text order, images at serving resolution (about 1,280 tokens), inputs carrying real yolo26 detections (plus false-candidate negatives), labels = flagship output under the rubric, rejection-sampled against declared truth, risk_score targets at or below band midpoint. Trainer: ms-swift or TRL (HF-native; Automodel if sm_103 needs NGC). Run a 20-step dry run first to measure peak VRAM (estimate: 8B QLoRA about 9-15 GiB, 8B bf16 LoRA about 24-27 GiB at 1 still, so it fits the ~40 GiB session cap). Export by merge in bf16 -> convert_hf_to_gguf -> llama-quantize Q4_K_M; reuse the shipped Q8_0 mmproj unchanged; do NOT run runtime --lora on b7972 (the 2026-02-14 LoRA fix postdates it). Evaluate only through the shipped replay/VlmClient path.
GATE G2 (discard otherwise): out-of-scenario S3 improves by >= 15 points over the best Stage 0/1 arm, S2 does not worsen, real-frame spot check does not regress, and llama.cpp-served results match HF-served within noise.

STAGE 3 (per tier, DECISION D3: how many tiers justify the maintenance of an adapter + GGUF + mmproj + eval each): reuse the dataset and policy layer; fine-tune only tiers whose base fails the gates, smallest first (4B for 10-12 GB), then 8B (16-24 GB), larger dense for 32-48 GB; 30B-A3B needs 80 GB-class training and is outside the 40 GiB cap.
STAGE 4 (conditional): DPO from existing conservative-vs-correct pairs; GRPO with the rule-based reward only if SFT overfits the FLUX domain, at LoRA rank <= 32.
STAGE 5: training a VLM from scratch is out of reach (about 2.2T multimodal tokens on top of a 36T-token LLM; 475 stills) and is not recommended.

**Smallest next step.** On the GB300 (no GPU, no download, under an hour): load /synthbench/runs/scores/20260930T031706Z/results.jsonl and compute, for both qwen3-vl-8b and the flagship, (1) per-scenario risk_score quantiles, (2) AUROC of risk_score for incident vs benign, and (3) the scenario-grouped-CV S3/S2 frontier achievable by a monotone remap of the raw score. In the same sitting make one served call on the pinned b7972 ai-vlm to log the JSON key emission order and whether logprobs come back. Those two results decide whether Stage 1 (calibrator) can close the gap or whether Stage 0.3 (prompt ladder + criteria aggregator replay arms, 18m43s per 8B run) is the next experiment.

**Findings.**

- **0. Headline: the measured S3 gap looks like a severity-POLICY gap, not a perception gap** - [V] The shipped prompt defines no scale: it says only 'how threatening it is (risk_score 0-100)' (backend/services/vlm_client.py:538-541) - no band edges, no anchors. In replay the VLM is also handed the event's declared subjects and props as ideal detections at confidence 1.0 (synthbench/export/vss.py:71-75; P5a spec decision A6). Even so, P5a (docs/benchmarks/synthbench/p5a-2026-09-30.md:20-27, 109-140, 222-262): both models confirm the declared objects on 96-98% of stills, yet S3 is 36.5% [30.7-42.8] (n=241) for Qwen3-VL-8B and 58.9% for the flagship. Per-scenario S3 is a cliff, not a uniform shift: 8B firearm_visible 94.7%, forced_entry 84.2%, masked_intruder_night 84.2% vs child_alone_at_pool 0.0%, package_theft 0.0%, fire_or_smoke 52.6%, knife_visible 52.6%. The flagship (179,999,981,459 params [E], HF API) still gets package_theft 5.3% [0.9-24.6] (n=19) while scoring masked_intruder_night 100%. [V] The S3 floor is the level of the declared band's midpoint (backend/evaluation/levels.py:49-62; eval_store.py:412-417): package_theft band (60,90) -> floor 'high'; child_alone_at_pool (85,100) -> 'critical'; loitering (40,70) -> 'medium'. [C] Same objects confirmed + per-scenario cliffs + a 180B model failing the same scenario as the 8B = disagreement with the corpus author's severity ladder, which no amount of extra perception capacity fixes. Consequences: (a) the cheapest fix is to put the ladder in the prompt or in code (section 12), not in weights; (b) a single global score shift cannot fix it, because S3 needs an ORDINAL ranking of scenarios (loitering >= medium, firearm >= critical, hooded_jogger < medium) and S2/S3 trade off scenario by scenario (flagship hard-negative flashlight_neighbor S2 24.1% vs 8B 3.4%); (c) a fine-tune on these labels would mostly teach this 31-scenario severity ladder, and the owner must confirm that ladder is the product's real policy before encoding it anywhere.
  - Evidence: backend/services/vlm_client.py:538-541; synthbench/export/vss.py:71-75; docs/benchmarks/synthbench/p5a-2026-09-30.md:20-27,109-140,222-262; backend/evaluation/levels.py:49-62; backend/evaluation/eval_store.py:412-417; https://huggingface.co/api/models/Qwen/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (safetensors.total=179999981459, usedStorage=360065103050); /synthbench/corpus/tierb-v0/events/B/\*/spec.json (risk_band per scenario)
- **1. Tooling: ms-swift (modelscope/ms-swift)** - [E] Apache-2.0, 15,775 stars, pushed 2026-10-02, v4.0 released 2026-03-03. Qwen3-VL has a first-class best-practice doc (docs/source_en/BestPractices/Qwen3-VL-Best-Practice.md): needs transformers>=4.57 and ms-swift>=4.0; custom data is JSONL with 'messages' plus an 'images' list and one <image> tag per image (multi-image supported); the assistant message is plain text, so our verdict JSON is just a stringified assistant turn (no special structured-output support needed [C]). Flags: --freeze_vit true --freeze_aligner true, --tuner_type lora, --target_modules all-linear, packing/padding_free, IMAGE_MAX_TOKEN_NUM env for image tokens (all examples use 1024), --use_logits_to_keep / liger kernel suggested by maintainers for long-seq OOM (issue #9112). Also DPO/KTO/GRPO-family (GRPO/DAPO/GSPO, vLLM rollouts) and swift export. Dense models via the transformers backend; MoE via Megatron-SWIFT. (license: Apache-2.0 | fit: Best documented Qwen3-VL path; pure PyTorch/transformers so the most likely to run on the GB300 (arm64, sm_103) [?: not tested here].)
  - Evidence: https://api.github.com/repos/modelscope/ms-swift; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/modelscope/ms-swift/main/docs/source_en/BestPractices/Qwen3-VL-Best-Practice.md; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/modelscope/ms-swift/main/README.md (lines 54-80); https://github.com/modelscope/ms-swift/issues/9112
- **2. Tooling: LLaMA-Factory (hiyouga/LlamaFactory)** - [E] Apache-2.0, 75,288 stars, pushed 2026-09-28. Qwen3-VL 2B/4B/8B/30B/32B/235B, template qwen3*vl. examples/train_lora/qwen3vl_lora_sft.yaml: Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct, LoRA rank 8, lora_target all, cutoff_len 2048, image_max_pixels 262144 (about 256 vision tokens, far below our ~1,280 per still), bf16, bs1 x GA8, lr 1e-4, 3 epochs; qwen3vl_lora_dpo.yaml exists (pref_beta 0.1). Data: ShareGPT/messages JSON with <image> tags and an images list (data/mllm_demo.json). QLoRA: bnb 4/8-bit, but the shipped QLoRA examples are text-only Qwen3-4B (no Qwen3-VL QLoRA example found in the examples tree). Its README hardware table is generic text-LLM and marked '\_estimated*': LoRA-16bit 7B 16GB / 14B 32GB / 30B 64GB; QLoRA-4bit 6GB / 12GB / 24GB. (license: Apache-2.0 | fit: Config-file driven, easiest first run; weakest on documented VLM memory numbers.)

- **3. Tooling: Unsloth** - [E] Apache-2.0, 77,161 stars, pushed 2026-10-03. Docs: Qwen3-VL (8B) SFT notebook and GRPO/GSPO notebook; 'Multi-image training' section (build the conversation list with a plain list comprehension instead of dataset.map); FastVisionModel.get_peft_model has finetune_vision_layers / finetune_language_layers / attention / mlp toggles; UnslothVisionDataCollator; train_on_responses_only; 'Saving Qwen3-VL to GGUF now works'. Requirements page: Python 3.11-3.13, CUDA capability >=7.0, CUDA 12.8+ for Blackwell (RTX 50). Two traps for our serving path (see section 9): the doc's example sets modules_to_save=['lm_head','embed_tokens'] (llama.cpp's LoRA converter rejects adapters carrying those), and finetune_vision_layers defaults to True (ViT LoRA tensors are silently dropped by the GGUF adapter converter). MoE: 'Qwen3-30B-A3B (16-bit LoRA) uses 63GB'; 4-bit MoE QLoRA not recommended because bitsandbytes does not support it. (license: Apache-2.0 | fit: Lowest VRAM per run (best for 12-24 GB cards) but custom kernels + bitsandbytes + xformers on the GB300 (aarch64, sm_103) are unverified [?].)

- **4. Tooling: TRL + PEFT (huggingface/trl)** - [E] Apache-2.0, 19,442 stars, pushed 2026-10-03. SFTTrainer 'fully supports' VLMs: dataset with an 'images' column (list per sample); set max_length=None for VLMs because truncation can remove image tokens; assistant-only loss on a vision dataset needs transformers>=5.18.0; PEFT LoraConfig integration; chat templates trl/chat_templates/qwen3_vl.jinja and qwen3_vl_training.jinja; HF cookbook has VLM SFT, DPO, GRPO, MPO notebooks (the SFT one is Qwen2-VL-7B on ChartQA, QLoRA, 'will utilize an A100' on Colab). TRL's 'LoRA Without Regret' guide (from Thinking Machines): apply LoRA to all linear layers (attention-only underperforms even at higher rank); rank 256 for post-training-scale SFT, but RL needs only rank 1-32; effective batch <32; LR about 10x full-FT. Those SFT figures are for 100K-example datasets; at our few-thousand-example scale rank 16-64 is ample [C]. (license: Apache-2.0 | fit: Most transparent and portable; you own the collator, loss masking and prompt templating (important for train/serve parity, section 10).)

Tooling: Axolotl and the official Qwen recipe** - [E] Axolotl (Apache-2.0, 12,513 stars): docs/multimodal.qmd lists Qwen3-VL (example base_model Qwen/Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct) but says 'Multimodal support is limited and doesn't have full feature parity' and 'sample_packing: false # not yet supported with multimodal'; LoRA example restricts lora_target_modules to model.language_model.layers (leaves ViT frozen). Official QwenLM/Qwen3-VL qwen-vl-finetune (Apache-2.0; repo last pushed 2026-01-30): HF Trainer + DeepSpeed, conversations JSON with <image> tags (one tag per file), defaults to full-parameter (tune_mm_llm True, tune_mm_vision False); scripts/sft_qwen3_4b.sh uses zero3, bs4 x GA4, model_max_length 8192, but max_pixels 50176 (224x224, about 64 tokens - nowhere near our 1,280); sft_30a3b_lora.sh comment: 'this script could run on 32 80G GPU' (MoE supports only zero2). Pins: torch 2.6.0, transformers 4.57.0.dev0, peft 0.17.1, deepspeed 0.17.1, flash_attn 2.7.4.post1 (old pins; will not drop onto a new Blackwell stack unchanged [C]). Fine-tuning VRAM: every number I actually read** - [E] A1 Unsloth Qwen3-VL-8B 4-bit QLoRA notebook (stored output, Tesla T4 14.741 GB, Unsloth 2025.10.2): 7.66 GB reserved after load; peak 8.213 GB reserved over 30 steps; r=16, bs2 x GA4, max*length 2048, 512px images; 214.98 s for 240 samples. A2 ms-swift Qwen3-VL-4B LoRA bf16 r=8, ViT+aligner frozen, ZeRO-2, bs1+packing, max_length 4096, IMAGE_MAX_TOKEN_NUM=1024: '2 * 21GiB' (per GPU). A3 ms-swift Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B LoRA bf16 r=8, ZeRO-3, 2 GPUs, max*length 2048, bs16/GPU: '2 * 42GiB'. A4 Unsloth: Qwen3-30B-A3B 16-bit LoRA 63GB (bnb 4-bit MoE unsupported). A5 Unsloth table ('absolute minimum', text LLM): 8B QLoRA 6GB / LoRA16 22GB; 14B 8.5GB / 33GB; 27B 22GB / 64GB; 32B 26GB / 76GB. A6 Unsloth Llama-3.1-8B QLoRA r=32 bs1 max context, Unsloth vs HF+FA2 (vendor benchmark, text-only): 12GB 21,848 vs 932; 16GB 40,724 vs 2,551; 24GB 78,475 vs 5,789; 48GB 191,728 vs 15,502 tokens. A7 Qwen3-VL issue #1687: full-FT 8B on 4x40GB A100 OOMed at 36.26 GiB in use per GPU. Real bf16 blob sizes [E, HF API/file listing]: Qwen3-VL-4B 8,875,719,344 B (8.27 GiB); 8B 17,534,339,512 B (16.33 GiB); 30B-A3B 62,141,618,016 B (57.87 GiB); Gemma-4-12B-it model.safetensors 23,919,549,408 B (22.28 GiB); Qwen3.5-9B 19,319,118,862 B (17.99 GiB). [C] Qwen3-VL-8B text tower from fetched config.json: hidden 4096, intermediate 12288, 36 layers, 32 heads / 8 KV heads, head_dim 128, vocab 151,936; ViT 27 layers x hidden 1152 = about 0.41B params (about 0.8 GiB bf16, frozen). LoRA params = r x 75,776 x 36 = 43.6M at r=16 (0.5% of 8.77B), 174.6M at r=64.

- **8. What that means for the 10-48 GB tiers and the GB300 (training-time estimates; training happens once on one machine, NOT per serving tier)** - Sequence budgets [C]: one still = 1,280 image tokens (repo constant, vlm_client.py:\_IMAGE_TOKENS_PER_FRAME) + about 800 prompt text [?] + about 600 verdict = about 2.7K tokens; four stills (MAX_KEY_FRAMES=4) = 5,120 + 800 + 850 (longest verdict seen: 852 tokens) = about 6.8K; the repo documents a worst-case fitted prompt of 13,785 served tokens. The tierb-v0 corpus has ONE still per event (445 of 460) so multi-image behavior cannot be trained from it. Estimates [?] (formula: bf16 blob + overhead, overhead anchored to A2: 21 GiB - 8.27 GiB = 12.7 GiB at 4,096 tokens, assumed to scale about linearly with tokens; logits over a 151,936 vocab dominate it and shrink with logits_to_keep / Liger / chunked CE): (a) 8B QLoRA 4-bit: about 9-11 GiB at 1 still, about 11-15 GiB at 4 stills (anchors A1, A6) -> fits 12 GB for 1-still, 16 GB for 4-still; (b) 4B QLoRA: about 5-7 GiB -> the only option that clearly fits a 10 GB card; (c) 4B bf16 LoRA: about 14-16 GiB (1 still) / 22-27 GiB (4 stills); (d) 8B bf16 LoRA: about 24-27 GiB (1 still) / 36-42 GiB (4 stills) -> 1-still needs a 32 GB card or more, 4-still needs 48 GB or logits tricks; (e) ~12B bf16 LoRA (Gemma-4-12B 22.28 GiB weights): about 33-37 GiB at 1 still -> 48 GB card; QLoRA about 11-14 GiB; (f) 30B-A3B: bf16 weights 57.87 GiB, Unsloth measured 63GB for 16-bit LoRA, ms-swift 2x42GiB with ZeRO-3 -> needs an 80 GB-class card or 2x48 GB sharded; QLoRA unsupported by bnb for MoE; does NOT fit a 40 GiB session. Under the agent-gpu runner's ~40 GiB cap (from the task brief, not verified by me): 8B QLoRA yes, 8B bf16 LoRA at 1-still examples yes (about 24-27 GiB [?]), 4B bf16 LoRA yes, 12B bf16 LoRA marginal, 30B-A3B no (GB300 outside the cap only). The GB300 is not VRAM-limited for any of these; the open question there is software (aarch64/sm_103 wheels for bitsandbytes/xformers/flash-attn) [?]. Time [?]: the T4 run did 240 samples in 215 s at <=2,048 tokens; a 3,000-sample x 3-epoch run at about 2.7K tokens is about 24M tokens, which is hours on one modern GPU, not days. First action before any commitment: a 20-step dry run that prints torch.cuda.max_memory_reserved at the real sequence length.

- **9. Serving route A: keep the adapter separate (llama.cpp --lora) - works for the text tower only, and the PINNED BUILD HAS A KNOWN LoRA BUG** - [E] llama.cpp supports it: convert_lora_to_gguf.py converts a PEFT adapter (--base <HF config dir>, --outtype f32|f16|bf16|q8_0) using the TEXT model class (Qwen3VLTextModel is registered for Qwen3VLForConditionalGeneration in conversion/qwen3vl.py:280-292); llama-server takes --lora FNAME / --lora-scaled FNAME:SCALE / --lora-init-without-apply, a per-request 'lora':[{id,scale}] field, and GET/POST /lora-adapters; 'requests with different LoRA configurations will not be batched together'. Limits read in the converter source: (i) vision tensors are dropped silently (TextModel.filter_tensors skips 'visual.'/'vision.'/'patch_embed' names, conversion/base.py:1389-1401) so a LoRA that touches the ViT converts WITHOUT its ViT part -> served model differs from trained model; (ii) a non-LoRA tensor in the adapter (embed_tokens / lm_head from modules_to_save) makes the script exit with an error (convert_lora_to_gguf.py ~470-480). So: train LLM linear layers only, ViT and aligner frozen (ms-swift --freeze_vit/--freeze_aligner; Unsloth finetune_vision_layers=False), no modules_to_save. The mmproj file is untouched, so the shipped Q8_0 mmproj (752,289,728 B) is reused byte-for-byte [C]. THE BUG: [E] issue #19217 (llama-server LoRA: infinite graph rebuild / continuous memory reserve / unbounded RAM growth, regression between b7692 and b7792, reproduced on b7856) was fixed by PR #19280 'only reset LoRa configs when they have changed from previous batch', merged 2026-02-14 (merge commit 2d8015e8). Our pin is b7972, commit e06088da0, published 2026-02-08; the GitHub compare API says 2d8015e8 is 77 commits AHEAD of e06088da0, so b7972 contains the regression and lacks the fix. Issue #21552 (b8477/b8683, RAM growth when changing LoRA scales at runtime, uses --mmproj + 3 adapters on Qwen3.5-0.8B) was closed stale 2026-05-22 with no fix referenced. Latest llama.cpp release is v0.5.0 (2026-09-23). Quality caveat [C]: an adapter trained against a bf16 (or bnb-NF4) base is applied at runtime on top of a Q4_K_M base, a different quantization error than it saw in training. (fit: Runtime LoRA is attractive for A/B (scale 0 vs 1 per request) but should not be used on b7972; bump the pin first or use route B.)
  - Evidence: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/master/convert_lora_to_gguf.py; .../conversion/qwen3vl.py:280-292; .../conversion/base.py:1389-1401; .../tools/server/README.md lines 94-95, 243, 603, 1199-1235; https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/19217; https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/19280; https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/21552; https://api.github.com/repos/ggml-org/llama.cpp/compare/e06088da0...2d8015e8a460f51a4c0fc8b1b9f41e38d7ea2194 (status=ahead, ahead_by=77); https://api.github.com/repos/ggml-org/llama.cpp/releases/tags/b7972 (published 2026-02-08); docs/benchmarks/synthbench/p5a-2026-09-30.md:60 (build b7972-e06088da0)
- **10. Serving route B (recommended): merge in bf16, then convert and quantize; plus train/serve parity warnings** - [E] Route: PEFT merge_and_unload (Unsloth: save_pretrained_merged(..., save_method='merged_16bit')) -> convert_hf_to_gguf.py -> llama-quantize Q4_K_M, same recipe the shipped GGUF used. Qwen3-VL mmproj conversion is supported in code (Qwen3VLVisionModel, conversion/qwen3vl.py:16-80; 'convert_hf_to_gguf.py --mmproj'); only regenerate it if the ViT/merger were trained. Do NOT use llama-export-lora on the Q4_K_M GGUF: it dequantizes the base to F32, merges, and 'output is forced to f16 for now' (tools/export-lora/export-lora.cpp:190, 315-322), and rejects quantized adapters (line 305) - that path double-quantizes. Serving VRAM is unchanged by route B (same quant size as the base GGUF: 5,027,784,800 B for the 8B) and about +87 MiB for a runtime r=16 adapter (43.6M params x 2 B [C]), so S1 is not affected either way. PARITY: [E] open issue ggml-org/llama.cpp#29251 (2026-09-21, Qwen3-VL-8B LoRA-merged fine-tune: 31/58 in HF transformers vs 22-24/58 in llama.cpp mtmd) was then partly RETRACTED by its author: the gap was a harness bug - message content order [text,image] vs [image,text] moved scores by about 30 points; the real residual is that mtmd's flash-attn path casts K/V to F16 (attn_out-0 error 4.9e-3 vs 1.07e-4 with -fa off) but with aligned protocol -fa on/off differ by <=1/58. Lessons [C]: (1) evaluate every candidate through the SHIPPED path (synthbench replay -> VlmClient -> llama.cpp + mmproj), never in HF; (2) train targets in the exact order the grammar emits: [V] the generated schema's properties are alphabetical (criteria, description, provenance, reasoning, risk_score, summary, verdict - backend/ai_contract/schemas/vlm_assess.response.json:48-86) and [E] llama.cpp's grammar builder emits required properties in schema order (common/json-schema-to-grammar.cpp:\_build_object_rule), so verdict is emitted LAST, after risk_score [?: confirm on b7972 with one served call]; an SFT target serialized verdict-first would be forced into a different order at serve time; (3) same chat template, system message, image-then-text content order and image resolution (about 1,280 tokens per still) in training and serving; (4) the 'provenance' fields are copied from the served model's identity (client instruction) - mask them from the loss and decide what model_id a merged model reports [?].

- **11. Serving route C: vLLM (>=24 GB tiers; separate stack)** - [E] vLLM's supported-models table lists Qwen3VLForConditionalGeneration (Qwen3-VL) and Qwen3VLMoeForConditionalGeneration (30B-A3B) with the LoRA column checked; LoRA is supported on the language backbone of most multimodal models, tower/connector LoRA only 'experimentally'; adapters can be loaded/unloaded at runtime via /v1/load_lora_adapter with VLLM_ALLOW_RUNTIME_LORA_UPDATING=True and sized with --max-lora-rank. vLLM serves bf16/FP8 weights, not our Q4_K_M GGUF, so it is a different serving stack with different VRAM (8B bf16 = 16.33 GiB before KV) - plausible for 24 GB+ tiers, not for 10-16 GB. [V] The client is engine-agnostic (OPENAI_VLM; provenance.engine records the engine), and the flagship already runs on vLLM. (license: Apache-2.0)

- **12. Data a fine-tune needs, and the traps in OUR corpus** - [V] tierb-v0 (/synthbench/corpus/tierb-v0/index.jsonl folded by event_id): 460 events (459 ready, 1 failed): 241 incident over 19 scenarios, 210 benign over 12, 9 ambiguous (costume_weapon). Seven core incident scenarios have 19 events each, twelve have 9; five benign hard-negative scenarios have 29 each, the rest 9-10. 475 stills in total, ALL rendered by one model (flux2-dev fp8mixed, sha 863a82e4ff95..., p1-slate.json) with one camera model (camera_params default-v1); stills are 1920x1080 JPEGs made from 1280x720 PNG renders; 5 cameras, 5 lightings, 4 weathers, 7 property types. The label is the declared spec.risk_band, ONE fixed band per scenario (e.g. firearm_visible 85-100, package_theft 60-90, hooded_jogger 0-25); the owner's 60-still audit found scene/people/conditions 60/60 correct and the prop missing in 1 of 15 (p5a:28-30). Label format for SFT = the full verdict JSON (criteria[name,passed,evidence], description, provenance, reasoning, risk_score int 0-100, summary, verdict). Declared truth supplies verdict, band and objects but NOT the free text (description, reasoning, criteria evidence): either template it from the spec (risk: teaches evidence that is not in the pixels) or distill it from the flagship under a rubric prompt and keep only samples whose verdict matches AND whose score reaches the floor (rejection sampling; only about 59% of incidents pass today, so fix the rubric first). Count [?]: LIMA learned a response format from 1,000 curated examples [E, arXiv 2305.11206], so format needs hundreds; learning a 31-scenario severity ladder that generalizes plausibly needs thousands (1.5-3K stills, 50-100 per scenario [?]); [C] render cost is small: 8.1 s/still (provenance render_seconds) = 2.25 h per 1,000 stills, so volume is cheap and DIVERSITY and LICENSE are the constraints. TRAPS: (a) split by scenario, never by image: the band is a function of scenario and 9-29 events per scenario share a prompt template, so a random split lets the model memorize scenario->band and S3 then measures memorization; use grouped K-fold over the 31 scenarios (each fold with >=2 critical-floor, >=1 high, >=1 medium incident scenarios and >=2 hard-negative benign) plus a LOCKED set of 4-5 whole scenarios never used for prompt-writing, calibration or training, plus real frames from the owner's own cameras (never trained on first). (b) The replay prompt contains the event's declared objects as detections at confidence 1.0 (A6): train on that and the model can learn detections-text -> score and ignore pixels, then meet yolo26's misses and false candidates in production. Train on real detector output (run yolo26 on the stills) plus cases where the detector candidate is false (verdict 'rejected'). (c) One generator + one camera model invites style shortcuts: run a non-semantic baseline (image statistics, JPEG tables, prompt length -> label) before trusting any gain [?]. (d) The rubric text must name observable criteria, not scenario names (a scenario list is an answer key). (e) Four benign scenarios (116 events) have band upper = 30 = the 'medium' floor, so a score of exactly 30 is inside its declared band yet counts as an S2 false alarm; target <= band midpoint. (f) risk_score is trained by cross-entropy over digit tokens, which has no ordinal structure: use a band midpoint or narrow in-band value so outputs cluster, or move to a level enum (but levels.py:5 records the owner decision that the model never emits a level - a contract change). (g) Certification arithmetic [C, Wilson 95%]: S3>=90% with the lower bound >=90% needs 227/241 (94.2%) on the full incident set and 59/60 on a 60-item set; S2<=5% with the upper bound <=5% needs <=4 false alarms of 209 (1.9%); today's S2 6.7% [4.0-10.9] is 14/209. Whether the bar is point-estimate or interval is an owner call.
  - Evidence: /synthbench/corpus/tierb-v0/{corpus.json,index.jsonl,events/B/_/spec.json,events/B/_/provenance.json}; synthbench/generate/manifests/p1-slate.json:3-11; synthbench/export/vss.py:71-75,78-100; backend/evaluation/s_metrics.py:28-60; backend/evaluation/levels.py:1-20; docs/benchmarks/synthbench/p5a-2026-09-30.md:28-30; docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-synthbench-p5a-vlm-replay-design.md:31,65,307; https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.11206
- **13. Licensing of generated data, public data, and base weights** - GENERATED STILLS: [V] every tierb-v0 still is FLUX.2 [dev] and the exporter stamps 'FLUX.2 [dev] Non-Commercial License' on it (synthbench/export/vss.py:40). [E] The flux2 repo's model_licenses/LICENSE-FLUX-NON-COMMERICAL (FLUX Non-Commercial License v2.1; the HF card names 'flux-non-commercial-license' but the gated README returned nothing, so I cannot confirm the owner-accepted text is this version [?]): 1(c) says use 'to train, fine tune, or distill other models for commercial use' is NOT a Non-Commercial Purpose; 2(d) says Outputs may be used 'for any purpose (including for commercial purposes)' except to 'train, fine-tune, or distill a model that is competitive with a FLUX Model'; 2(e) requires content filtering or output review and AI disclosure; commercial licenses via bfl.ai/licensing. A security VLM is not an image generator, so 2(d) probably permits it, but 1(c) and 2(d) read in tension for a commercial product: counsel/owner decision [?]. The owner's standing ruling D8 ('licenses are not a selection criterion') was made for benchmarking, not for training a shipped model. MiniMax-H3 clips: [E] MiniMax-H3 Community License V.3: 'You may not use the MiniMax H3 Works or any of their Outputs or results to improve any other artificial intelligence model'; V.4 and 'Excluded Territories' (EU, UK, Korea, USA): no use of the Works or Outputs outside the Applicable Territory. [V] the repo already records this (design spec lines 241-242). Conclusion [C]: H3 clips and frames taken from them must stay out of any training set; they remain usable only for the benchmark under the owner's earlier ruling. License-clean generators do not currently cover threats: [E] FLUX.2-klein-4B is Apache-2.0, FLUX.2-klein-9B is flux-non-commercial, Qwen-Image-2.1 is 'qwen-research', Wan2.2-I2V-A14B is Apache-2.0; [V] the P1 bake-off shows flux2-klein-4b failing handgun_in_hand, knife, crowbar_at_door and forced_door at 0% (p1-bakeoff.md:113). PUBLIC DATA: UCF-Crime has video-level (weak) labels only, no risk scores [E, arXiv 1801.04264 abstract]; HF mirror jinmang2/ucf_crime carries a 'cc0-1.0' tag (1,610 train + 290 test videos per its card) but that is the uploader's tag on third-party surveillance footage, not the originators' grant; the task brief says research-only and I could not verify it (www.crcv.ucf.edu did not respond). XD-Violence mirrors are tagged mit/apache-2.0 by their uploaders, same caveat. Neither supplies our verdict JSON. BASE WEIGHTS: [E] Qwen3-VL-4B/8B/30B-A3B-Instruct are apache-2.0 (HF cardData), so fine-tuning, merging, GGUF conversion and redistribution of derivatives are permitted with the usual notices; Gemma-4-12B-it is tagged apache-2.0 with a link to Google's gemma_4_license page [?: read that page before redistributing]; the flagship teacher Qwen3.8-Flash-Next is under Qwen Community License 1.0 - I read the full 3.2 KB text: MIT-style permission to fine-tune and create derivatives, name display only above 100M MAU or US$20M monthly revenue, separate license for Model-as-a-Service / AI Work Assistant businesses, and NO clause restricting use of its outputs to train other models, so distilling its verdicts into the 8B is permitted by that text [E]. A single-user home product is not MaaS [C]. Not legal advice.
  - Evidence: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/black-forest-labs/flux2/main/model_licenses/LICENSE-FLUX-NON-COMMERICAL (sections 1.c, 2.d, 2.e); https://huggingface.co/api/models/black-forest-labs/FLUX.2-dev (cardData.license_name=flux-non-commercial-license, gated=auto); https://huggingface.co/MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3/raw/main/LICENSE (lines 8-10, 41-42); https://huggingface.co/api/models/{black-forest-labs/FLUX.2-klein-4B,black-forest-labs/FLUX.2-klein-9B,Qwen/Qwen-Image-2.1,Wan-AI/Wan2.2-I2V-A14B}; https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next/raw/main/LICENSE; https://huggingface.co/api/models/{Qwen/Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct,google/gemma-4-12B-it}; https://huggingface.co/api/datasets?search=ucf-crime; https://huggingface.co/datasets/jinmang2/ucf_crime/raw/main/README.md; https://arxiv.org/abs/1801.04264; synthbench/export/vss.py:40; docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-synthetic-benchmark-generation-design.md:43,241-242; docs/benchmarks/synthbench/p1-bakeoff.md:113
- **14. Cheaper fixes for a CALIBRATION problem (in order of cost), tied to our numbers** - A. Prompt severity ladder [V][C]: the shipped prompt has no scale (finding 0). Add the shipped bands (low 0-29, medium 30-59, high 60-84, critical 85-100, frontend RISK_THRESHOLDS per levels.py) with anchors defined by observable criteria (weapon in hand, forced entry in progress, concealed face + night + private zone, child unattended near water, ...) and an explicit rule for what to score when objects are confirmed but behavior is ambiguous. Cost: hours; one replay of the 450 stills took 18m43s on the 8B and 52m19s on the flagship (p5a:9-10). Expected effect is large because the 8B already follows the policy on 3 scenarios (firearm 94.7%) and fails on others for lack of one [C], but no source predicts a number [?]. B. Structured criteria scoring with a code aggregator: the schema already carries criteria[name,passed,evidence], emitted BEFORE risk_score in the alphabetical grammar order; today criterion names are free-form strings, so fix a closed criterion list in the prompt and compute the score from the booleans with a policy table kept in the repo (auditable, tunable without retraining, identical across all five hardware tiers). G-Eval's form-filling + CoT pattern is the published precedent (Spearman 0.514 with humans on summarization, arXiv 2303.16634). C. Post-hoc calibration layer: isotonic or logistic map from [raw risk_score, verdict, criteria pass-count, detector classes] to a calibrated score, fit with scenario-grouped CV (Guo et al. 2017, arXiv 1706.04599: post-processing calibration for poorly calibrated modern nets; 100s of examples suffice for a few parameters [C]). It fixes bias/scale, NOT mis-ranked scenarios, so first compute the AUROC of raw risk_score for incident vs benign and the scenario-grouped-CV S3/S2 frontier of the best monotone remap from the existing P5a results.jsonl (on the GB300; not in this sandbox): high AUROC (about 0.9+ [?]) means a calibrator can clear the bars; low AUROC means discrimination is the problem. D. Verbalized scores are usually better calibrated than token probabilities for RLHF models (Tian et al. 2023, arXiv 2305.14975, about 50% lower ECE) and models can be taught calibrated verbal uncertainty (Lin et al. 2022, arXiv 2205.14334); logprob-weighted scoring needs the server to return logprobs on b7972 [?]. E. Self-consistency / ensembling: replay averaged 1,123 s / 450 = 2.5 s per item [C] (concurrency unknown) against S4 = 30 s p95, so 3-5 samples fit [?], but averaging reduces variance, not the systematic 'confirmed objects, scored low' bias, so it does not fix S3 [C]. F. Two-pass: pass 1 verifies and describes (images), pass 2 is a text-only severity call over description + criteria + household context against the ladder, same loaded model, no images, so about 1-2K tokens [C]; moves policy into text where the 8B is strong. G. Few-shot with images: each exemplar costs about 1,280 tokens inside a 16,384-token slot that must already hold up to 5,120 image tokens; prefer text-only anchors.
  - Evidence: backend/services/vlm_client.py:538-548; backend/evaluation/levels.py:1-47; backend/ai_contract/schemas/vlm_assess.response.json:48-86; docs/benchmarks/synthbench/p5a-2026-09-30.md:9-10,20-27; https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.16634; https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.04599; https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.14975; https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.14334
- **15. When SFT, DPO or GRPO becomes worth it** - [C] Fine-tune only after rungs A-C of finding 14 are measured on out-of-scenario folds, and only for failures that survive them: (1) perception/discrimination errors with the correct ladder in the prompt on HELD-OUT scenarios (e.g. child_alone_at_pool or knife_visible still below floor, hard negatives like hooded_jogger still alarming); (2) a small-tier model (4B for 10-12 GB) that cannot follow the ladder at all; (3) real-footage frames where the big model works and the small one does not. SFT = distillation of the flagship (teacher, rubric prompt, rejection-sampled against declared truth) into the 8B/4B: that is where fine-tuning pays most, because the student gets the teacher's perception and the product's policy at 5 GB. Evidence on side effects [E]: 'LoRA Learns Less and Forgets Less' (arXiv 2405.09673): LoRA underperforms full fine-tuning in 100K-example / 20B-token regimes but forgets less and keeps base capability, and full-FT learns perturbations of rank 10-100x typical LoRA configs - our data is orders of magnitude smaller, so capacity is not the limit and LoRA's low forgetting is the useful property. DPO: preference pairs are nearly free here (same still: rubric-correct vs conservative output from the existing replays; LLaMA-Factory ships qwen3vl_lora_dpo.yaml, ms-swift/TRL support DPO) but the failure is largely a 1-D bias, which a calibrator fixes more cheaply; use DPO if SFT leaves discrimination errors [?]. GRPO: our reward is rule-based and verifiable (schema valid, verdict matches, score >= floor / in band, penalty on S2), which is the setting VLM-R1 (arXiv 2504.07615) reports RL generalizing better than SFT, with reward hacking seen in detection; TRL's guide says RL needs only LoRA rank 1-32; Unsloth has a Qwen3-VL-8B GRPO notebook and ms-swift has GRPO/vLLM rollouts. Cost is rollouts at >=1,280 vision tokens x group size over a few hundred prompts, so it is Stage 4 at best, only if SFT overfits the FLUX domain.
  - Evidence: https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09673; https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.07615; https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01785; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/huggingface/trl/main/docs/source/lora_without_regret.md (lines 259-318); https://raw.githubusercontent.com/hiyouga/LlamaFactory/main/examples/train_lora/qwen3vl_lora_dpo.yaml; https://unsloth.ai/docs/models/tutorials/qwen3-how-to-run-and-fine-tune/qwen3-vl-how-to-run-and-fine-tune.md (lines 326-345)
- **16. One adapter per base model: what 'a different VLM per RTX tier' costs under a fine-tune strategy** - [C] A LoRA is tied to its base architecture (delta shapes follow hidden/intermediate size: 8B text tower hidden 4096 / intermediate 12288 [E config]; the 4B is a different size, and the 12B-class Gemma 4 a different family), so five tiers with five different bases = five training runs, five GGUF/mmproj pairs, five eval passes, and five llama.cpp architecture-support checks (Gemma 4 LoRA/mmproj handling in llama.cpp not verified [?]). The reusable asset is the DATASET and the policy layer, not the adapter. Therefore: build the policy layer (rubric + criteria aggregator + calibrator) once so every tier inherits it; fine-tune only the tiers whose base model still fails the gates, expected to be the smallest (4B) first. Does more hardware mean better scores? In our data a 180B teacher gains +22 S3 points over the 8B (58.9% vs 36.5%) but shares the same per-scenario cliffs, so size alone does not clear 90% until the policy is explicit [V][C].
  - Evidence: https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct/raw/main/config.json; https://huggingface.co/api/models/google/gemma-4-12B-it; docs/benchmarks/synthbench/p5a-2026-09-30.md:20-27; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Automodel/main/README.md (Gemma 4 line)
- **17. Training our own model from scratch: out of reach, and it would not help** - [E] Qwen3-VL is not trained from scratch either: its report (arXiv 2511.21631, HTML) lists stages S0 vision-language alignment 67B tokens, S1 multimodal pre-training about 1T, S2 long-context about 1T, S3 ultra-long adaptation 100B (about 2.17T multimodal tokens) on top of an LLM line (Qwen3) pre-trained on about 36T tokens (arXiv 2505.09388), with a training framework described as scaling to 10,000 GPUs. [C] 6 x 8.77e9 params x 2.17e12 tokens = 1.14e23 FLOPs for the VL stages alone, about 18 GPU-years at an ASSUMED sustained 2e14 FLOP/s per GPU [?: my assumption]; a LoRA run on 3,000 samples x 3 epochs x 2.7K tokens = 24M tokens is about 1e-5 of that token budget (27M/2.17T = 1.2e-5). Our corpus is 475 stills from one generator. LLaVA-1.5's much-cited 'about 1 day on a single 8-A100 node' (arXiv 2310.03744) is for a 13B model assembled from a pretrained CLIP ViT and a pretrained LLM plus 1.2M public samples - that is adaptation, and it would yield a worse general VLM than the Qwen3-VL we already run. Conclusion: training a VLM from scratch is out of reach and unnecessary. The realistic 'own model' options are (i) a distilled LoRA/merged student on open weights (Stages 2-3 below) and (ii) a small specialist head or probe trained on frozen features for one narrow decision (for example risk level from the VLM's criteria booleans plus detector classes), which is the calibrator of finding 14.C.
  - Evidence: https://arxiv.org/html/2511.21631 (training-stage table, 'approximately 1 trillion (1T) tokens', 'up to 10,000 GPUs'); https://arxiv.org/html/2505.09388 ('36 trillion tokens'); https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.03744; https://huggingface.co/api/models/Qwen/Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct (8,767,123,696 params)
- **18. Question 1 (benchmark): we already have the benchmark; what it lacks is protocol and real frames** - [V] The 'simple security benchmark' exists: synthbench tierb-v0 + the P5a replay through the shipped VlmClient (docs/benchmarks/synthbench/p5a-2026-09-30.md; python -m synthbench doctor/export/audit/replay/score per the handoff). What it lacks for a fine-tune decision: (1) a scenario-grouped CV protocol and a locked scenario set (finding 12a); (2) real frames from the owner's own cameras as a never-trained-on test (the corpus is 100% synthetic by owner ruling D3; real footage only calibrates camera noise, D13); (3) a real-detector arm (A6 says S3 may read higher than production). Public datasets I checked supply no risk labels (finding 13). Harbor was not investigated in this track.
  - Evidence: docs/benchmarks/synthbench/p5a-2026-09-30.md:42-60; docs/plans/2026-10-03-vss-vlm-exercise-handoff.md:20-30; docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-synthetic-benchmark-generation-design.md:36-45; docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-synthbench-p5a-vlm-replay-design.md:31

**Not verified.**

- Harbor was not investigated in this track (owner Q1 mentions it); another track presumably covers existing benchmarks.
- No fetched source predicts the S3/S2 gain of a LoRA (or of a prompt rubric) on this task; every 'expected effect' statement is an inference [C], not a measurement. The decision has to be empirical (Stage 0 gates).
- No measured peak VRAM for LoRA/QLoRA training of Qwen3-VL-8B/4B/12B-class at 1,280 vision tokens per still, 1 or 4 stills per sample: the only VLM measurements found are Unsloth's T4 run (<=2,048 tokens, 512px images), ms-swift's 4B example (4,096 tokens) and the 30B-A3B example (2,048 tokens). The tier table is an estimate [?] (bf16 blob + overhead scaled linearly in tokens) and needs a 20-step dry run.
- Training throughput on the GB300 and the ~40 GiB agent-gpu session cap were not measured or verified (the cap is from the task brief). Whether bitsandbytes/xformers/flash-attn/Unsloth kernels run on aarch64 sm_103 is unverified; NeMo Automodel's Qwen3-VL-8B recipe is full-parameter FSDP2, and I did not confirm a Qwen3-VL-8B LoRA recipe there.
- llama.cpp: I read master, not the b7972 source. That b7972 (commit e06088da0, 2026-02-08) predates the LoRA fix PR #19280 (merged 2026-02-14, 77 commits ahead per the compare API) is verified; the actual magnitude of the b7972 LoRA symptom on our CUDA path was not reproduced. I did not verify that b7972's convert_lora_to_gguf.py handles Qwen3VLForConditionalGeneration exactly as master does (Qwen3-VL llama.cpp support dates from 2025-10-30 per Unsloth docs). Gemma 4 12B LoRA/mmproj support in llama.cpp, ms-swift, LLaMA-Factory and Automodel is unconfirmed (Automodel lists Gemma 4 2B/4B/31B/26B-A4B).
- The JSON key emission order on b7972 is inferred from the on-disk schema (alphabetical, verdict last) plus master's grammar builder; it needs one served call to confirm. Whether b7972's /v1/chat/completions returns logprobs was not checked.
- FLUX.2 [dev] license: I read FLUX Non-Commercial License v2.1 from the black-forest-labs/flux2 GitHub repo; the HF model README is gated and returned nothing, so the exact text the owner accepted is unconfirmed, and the 1(c) vs 2(d) reading for a commercial product is a counsel question. Gemma 4's license page (ai.google.dev/gemma/docs/gemma_4_license) was not fetched; the HF card tags apache-2.0.
- UCF-Crime original usage terms: www.crcv.ucf.edu did not respond from the sandbox (curl exit/HTTP 000), so 'research-only' (from the task brief) is unverified; the 'cc0-1.0' and 'mit' tags on HF mirrors are uploader claims. No other public surveillance dataset with severity labels was evaluated.
- The 'about 800 prompt text tokens' per call used for sequence budgets is my estimate; the repo documents prompts up to 13,785 served tokens in one worst case.
- Whether the flagship's declared-band agreement improves with a rubric was not measured; the 59% of incidents that pass today bounds how many teacher samples survive rejection sampling before the rubric exists.
- The HF API was rate-limited several times (500 requests per 300 s per IP, shared with other agents); all model/dataset facts quoted were fetched successfully, but some searches were limited to a few calls and are not exhaustive.
- I did not run any model, GPU job, or conversion; the interaction of a merged-then-quantized LoRA with the shipped Q4_K_M + Q8_0-mmproj stack (quality drift vs bf16) is untested [?].

## 5. Completeness critic

**Verdict.** Not decision-ready as a ladder. The file sizes, licences and architecture facts I spot-checked all held (12 of 12 sets). Three things are wrong or open. (1) The "qwen35 absent at b8000" runtime evidence is invalid. (2) Every model pick is fit-only, with no accuracy evidence above Qwen3-VL-8B. (3) The tracks disagree on why S3 fails, and that decides whether any tier pick matters.

Answers to the owner's three questions:

- Q1 (benchmark, Harbor, LoRA, own model): answered well and consistently. tierb-v0 is the benchmark. Harbor is an agent-in-container harness and does not fit. No public set measures S2/S3 as defined. Training from scratch is out of reach. LoRA is conditional.
- Q2 (a model per card size): answered only as fit and support estimates. No candidate has tierb-v0 numbers.
- Q3 (more resources, more accuracy): answered "probably not mainly". The only evidence is the 8B-to-flagship jump (S3 36.5% to 58.9%). That jump is confounded by generation, size and thinking, and it is still far below 90%.

Best next action: the fit gate does not need a card of each size. The 24 GB A5500 is sm_86, the same as the 3080/3060/A6000, so whole-stack peak VRAM for every candidate up to roughly 20 GiB can be measured there now. Accuracy can be measured on the GB300.

The one thing to settle before choosing between tiers is whether S3 >= 90% is reachable on tierb-v0 at all. 64 of 241 incidents (26.6%) look like single-still-indistinguishable stranger/intent scenes, which caps S3 near 73% for any model.

missing:

1. Reconcile the S3 diagnosis; the tracks conflict.
   - The LoRA track says the miss is a severity-policy gap. Its Stage 0/1 plan (prompt ladder, then a calibrator) assumes the raw risk_score has good ranking power.
   - The size-vs-accuracy track measured the opposite: AUROC 0.70, best 42% TPR at 5% FPR, scores polarized (68% in {0,5,10}), and 64 of 241 incidents are twin-ambiguous. A recalibration therefore cannot reach 90%, and Stage 1 is largely foreclosed.
   - A prompt ladder that raises package_theft or loitering will likely raise S2 on delivery_driver and neighbor_passing.
   - A LoRA trained on declared per-scenario bands would memorize scenario to band on exactly those twin scenes.
   - The LoRA track's "STOP at gate G1" and the Q1 recommendation never say that the corpus itself, not the model, may cap S3. Fixing the corpus (multi-frame sets, relabeling) is listed only as a lever, not as a precondition for any fine-tune.
   - The owner needs this stated, plus a decision on whether the 90% bar is attainable on single stills.
2. No same-family size ladder on tierb-v0 (for example Qwen3.5 4B/9B/27B, or Qwen3-VL 4B/8B/32B) and no 2x2 of prompt by size. The E3 experiment is proposed but not run. Without it, the answer to "more VRAM gives better scoring?" has no controlled evidence.
3. No single llama.cpp pin is recommended.
   - Tier tracks cite b11090, b11376, b11246 or newer (Muse), and "after #29773" (Gemma).
   - The LoRA conversion fix #28324 is also after b11090.
   - The repo Dockerfile comments mention b11165.
   - A single pin of at least b11376, built for sm_86, sm_89 and sm_120 and qualified once against the Qwen3-VL-8B control, would serve every tier.
4. S4 is never quantified per card. No memory bandwidth or TFLOPS was fetched for any card; they are all "[A]" or from memory. Prefill for 4 x 1280 tokens on a 3060 or 4060 Ti is not addressed. S4 is the likeliest failure for the 12 GB 9B pick, the 16 GB Q6_K pick and any dense 27B.
5. No non-llama.cpp NVIDIA runtime was considered (TensorRT-LLM, NIM, Triton). The repo has docs/plans/triton-migration.md, and the product's owner is at NVIDIA. This is why the NVIDIA-lineage models (Nemotron 12B VL, Cosmos, Omni) were rejected almost entirely on llama.cpp's fixed 256-token projector. Whether another runtime changes that was not examined [?].
6. Candidates not sized or considered.
   - Dense Qwen3.6-27B (2.45M downloads, 2026-04-21) and Qwen3.5-27B (1.87M downloads) exist on HF, with GGUF, FP8, NVFP4 and AWQ repos. The 24 and 32 GB tiers consider only the newer Qwen3.8-27B, which has a thinner track record.
   - allenai Molmo2-8B/4B and nvidia Llama-3.1-Nemotron-Nano-VL-8B are never mentioned. They are probably not competitive, but the exclusion is not stated.
7. No "fewer models" strategy was evaluated. Options include one 4B-class model for 10-12 GB, one for 16-24 GB, and one for 32-48 GB, or one model with per-tier quantization and slot count. The LoRA track notes that a LoRA is tied to its base model (one adapter per base), but nobody costed 5-6 base models against 2-3.
8. Licensing is mostly flagged but not merged.
   - Track 2 says the FLUX.2-dev licence was unreadable and calls it a blocker. Track 3 read the GitHub LICENSE-FLUX-NON-COMMERICAL. I fetched it: section 1(c) says training or distilling other models for commercial use is not a Non-Commercial Purpose (it governs use of the FLUX Model, Derivatives and Content Filters), while section 2(d) lets Outputs be used commercially except to train a model competitive with FLUX. A VLM is probably not competitive with FLUX, so risk is low but needs counsel. The HF-gated text may be a different version and was not read.
   - Unread terms: Gemma 4 licence page, NVIDIA Open Model Agreement, MiniCPM Model License, Muse usage policy, MEVID licence, and the licences of the underlying MME-RealWorld image sets.
   - Harbor Hub (and any HF/lmms-eval registry) would share FLUX outputs publicly. Say so explicitly.
9. No real-data S3 estimate. The recommendation is MEVA (CC BY 4.0), but nobody counted how many person_steals_object or person_abandons_package intervals exist in the public 22 annotated hours. The UCF-Crime slice (140 incident videos) has unclear licensing and no risk bands. A real-frame S3 is therefore not yet supportable.
10. Which "Harbor" the owner meant is unresolved (harbor-framework/harbor versus av/harbor). Both are judged unsuitable, so this is minor.
11. Whether the S1 budget treats "GB" as GiB, and how much VRAM the driver or display reserves, is flagged only in the 16 GB tier. It is not flagged for the 10 GB fallback, whose margin is 0.04-0.31 GiB.

- Most recommended picks (Qwen3.5/3.6/3.8 hybrids at nearly every tier) sit in one crash matrix: #29629 open, #25717 closed not_planned (FA + q8_0 KV, sm_120), #28877 open (sm_120). The 16 and 32 GB tiers include sm_120 cards (5070 Ti/5080/5090) and none was tested. No diversified primary exists.
- 24 GB primary Gemma-4-26B-A4B QAT is fit-only: needs -ub above the shipped 512, json_schema issues #22396/#23990 closed not_planned and #23993 open, SWA KV figure assumed, 'bf16-like quality' is Google's own claim. Better framed as a lineage-control experiment arm than the primary.
- 16 GB ambitious pick (ISTA-DASLab 2-bit IQ2_S Qwen3.8-27B) is a community quant with text-only evals and unmeasured vision degradation; it conflicts with the 32 GB tier's own warning against IQ-class quants (#28877).
- 48 GB primary Qwen3.6-35B-A3B S4 advantage is inferred from the 'A3B' name only. Fallback Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B evidence is n=13 at a 700-token budget (9/13 truncated, 10 refusals). Dense 27B at Q8_0 (fits 48 GB) not weighed as a lower-risk alternative.
- Size-vs-accuracy evidence: '71% of misses argue non-threat' is a keyword heuristic; flagship group counts derive from rounded percentages not raw rows; 'confirmed' under the ideal detector is weak proof of perception (E6 ablation not run). The 73% ceiling assumes group A is unrecoverable from one still.
- LoRA-to-GGUF path: merge-then-quantize is sensible but adapters on qwen35 linear_attention projections are untested; quantizing a merged fine-tune may shift calibration. Public LoRA evidence is mixed (Hawkeye +26.6 pp self-reported in-distribution; Qwen3.8-27B UCF-Crime LoRA tripled false alarms).
- First-support build for qwen35 was never properly bisected; the 'absent at b8000' evidence is a 404 on a non-existent tag.
